But wait, is g7 safe from the Queen at a7? Yes, Queen is at a7, a7-g7 is the 7th rank.
So g7 is also a move?
Let's check the Queen at a7 again. It attacks the 7th rank (a7 to h7).
So if the King moves to g7, it is in check from the Queen at a7.
Therefore, g7 is illegal.
What about h5?
Rook at e6: attacks e6, e7, e8, e5, e4... and a6, b6, c6, d6, f6, g6, h6.
So h5 is safe from the Rook.
Is h5 safe from the Queen at a7?
Queen at a7: attacks rank 7, file a, and diagonals (a7-g1, a7-b8).
h5 is not on rank 7, not on file a, and not on the diagonals.
So h6h5 is a legal move.

What about any other piece?
Pawn at g5: cannot move if King is in check.
Pawn at h7: cannot move if King is in check.

So, the only legal move to escape the check from the Rook at e6 is h6h5 (or potentially g6, but g6 is attacked by the Rook).
Actually, let's re-verify. The Rook is at e6. It attacks the entire 6th rank.
King is at h6.
Moving to h5 removes the King from the 6th rank.
Moving to g6 keeps the King on the 6th rank (Still in check).
Moving to g7 puts the King on the 7th rank, where the Queen at a7 attacks.
So h6h5 is indeed the only legal move.

I will make the move h6h5.
